\mathfrak{X}^{*}
admits an equivalent
p
-smooth norm for some
p>1
(see
[
57
, Definition 10.18 and 10.30, and Theorem 10.25]
), and therefore
\mathfrak{X}^{*}
has type
p>1
(by
[
57
, Proposition 10.39]
); hence
\mathfrak{X}^{*}
is
K
-convex (see
[
40
, Theorem 7.4.23]
) and thus so is
\mathfrak{X}
(by
[
40
, Proposition 7.4.5]
).]

Having explained in Remark
10.3
why a soft construction of the weight is not available in general, we now turn to the details of the alternative approach that was also sketched above.

Proposition 10.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be an infinite-dimensional
K
-convex Banach space. Then there exists
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))
such that the Hilbert transform is not bounded on
L^{p}(V;\mathfrak{X})
.

Proof.

The construction of
V
involves a variation of the construction of
B
in Proposition
9.12
. By Corollary
9.8
, for every
k\geq 1
, the space
\mathfrak{X}
has a subspace
G_{k}
with the properties (
i
) and (
ii
) of Theorem
9.7
, and
\dim G_{k}=2N_{k}
can be taken
even
and as large as we like. Let
\iota_{k}
,
P_{k}
,
H_{k}
,
and
C
have the same meaning as in Theorem
9.7
with
G_{k}
in place of
G
; note that
C
depends only on
\mathfrak{X}
, and is hence independent of
k
. Since
\dim H_{k}=\dim G_{k}=2N
, we can write it as a direct sum
H_{k}=E_{k}\otimes E_{k}
of two copies of the same
N_{k}
-dimensional Hilbert space
E_{k}
.

Auxiliary
\operatorname{BMO}
functions

By Corollary
9.6
, there is a function

B_{k}\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))

with
B^{*}(=B)\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))
of norm at most one in these spaces such that

\|[B_{k},\mathcal{H}]\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{p}(\mathbb{R};E_{k}))}\gtrsim\log\dim E_{k}=\log N_{k}.

Replacing
B_{k}
by its translated and dilated version with the same estimates, as in the proof of Proposition
9.12
, we obtain the existence of
f_{k}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R};E_{k})
and
g_{k}\in L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R};E_{k})
of norm
1
, both supported in
J_{k}:=[4k,4k+1]
, such that

(10.6)

|\langle[B_{k},\mathcal{H}]f_{k},g_{k}\rangle|\gtrsim\log N_{k}.

Moreover, subtracting a constant, which does not affect the commutator nor the
\operatorname{BMO}
norms, we may assume that
\langle B_{k}\rangle_{J_{k}}=0
.

For each
h\geq 1
, we now apply the algorithm (
9.6
) to the sequence
\{b_{k}=\delta_{h,k}B_{h}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
to produce a function
b=:\tilde{B}_{h}
, which agrees with
B_{h}
on
J_{h}
, consists of Whitney averages of
B_{h}|_{J_{j}}
on the two adjacent unit intervals, and vanishes everywhere else. Applying Lemma
9.11
to each
b_{k}(\cdot)\mathfrak{e}
and
b_{k}(\cdot)^{*}\mathfrak{e}
, with
\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{E}\leq 1
, in place of
b_{k}
, we deduce that

(10.7)

\|\tilde{B}_{k}\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\lesssim 1,\quad\|\tilde{B}_{k}^{*}\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\lesssim 1,

while (
10.6
) still holds with
\tilde{B}_{k}
in place of
B_{k}
, since the supports of
f_{k}
and
g_{k}
ensure that the left-hand side only sees the values of
B_{k}
on
J_{k}
, where it agrees with
\tilde{B}_{k}
.

Initial weighs
\tilde{V}_{k}
on
H_{k}

We now define a weight
\tilde{V}_{k}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathscr{L}(H_{k})=\mathscr{L}(E_{k}\oplus E_{k})
by
setting, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

(10.8)

\tilde{V}_{k}(x):=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
\tilde{B}_{k}(x)&I\end{pmatrix}.

From (
10.7
) and Lemma
10.2
, we deduce that
\tilde{V}_{k}\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(H_{k}))
with

(10.9)

[\tilde{V}_{k}]_{\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(H_{k}))}\lesssim 1.

Since
\tilde{B}_{k}(x)\equiv 0
for
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus 3J_{k}
, it follows that

\tilde{V}_{k}(x)\equiv I\quad\forall\ x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus 3J_{k}.

Recall that
\tilde{B}_{k}=B_{k}
on
J_{k}
, while
\tilde{B}_{k}
is obtained from the Whitney averages of
B_{k}
on the two adjacent intervals
J_{k}\pm 1
. Hence, for each of these intervals
J\in\{J_{k},J_{k}\pm 1\}
, we have

\langle\tilde{B}_{k}\rangle_{J}=\langle B_{k}\rangle_{J_{k}}=0,

where the last identity was part of the construction of
B_{k}
.

By Lemma
10.2
again, we have the following additional special bounds

\begin{split}\frac{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J,\tilde{V}_{k}^{-*})}(\mathfrak{u})}{\|\mathfrak{u}\|_{H_{k}}}&\leq 1+\|\tilde{B}_{k}^{*}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}_{\operatorname{so}}(J;\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\\
&=1+\|\tilde{B}_{k}^{*}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}_{0,\operatorname{so}}(J;\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\qquad\text{since }\langle\tilde{B}_{k}^{*}\rangle_{J}=0\\
&\lesssim 1+\|\tilde{B}_{k}^{*}\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\lesssim 1\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,\tilde{V}_{k})}(\mathfrak{u})}{\|\mathfrak{u}\|_{H_{k}}}&\leq 1+\|\tilde{B}_{k}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}_{\operatorname{so}}(J;\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\\
&=1+\|\tilde{B}_{k}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}_{0,\operatorname{so}}(J;\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\qquad\text{since }\langle\tilde{B}_{k}\rangle_{J}=0\\
&\lesssim 1+\|\tilde{B}_{k}\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(E_{k}))}\lesssim 1;\end{split}

thus

(10.10)

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J,\tilde{V}_{k}^{-*})}(\mathfrak{u})+\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,\tilde{V}_{k})}(\mathfrak{u})\lesssim\|\mathfrak{u}\|_{H_{k}}\quad\text{for }J\in\{J_{k},J_{k}\pm 1\}.

Lifted weighs
V_{k}
on the original space
\mathfrak{X}

We now lift these weights to the original space
\mathfrak{X}
by setting

(10.11)

V_{k}:=I_{k}\oplus\iota_{k}^{-1}\tilde{V}_{k}\iota_{k}\quad\text{with respect to}\quad\mathfrak{X}=(I-P_{k})\mathfrak{X}\oplus P_{k}\mathfrak{X},

where
I_{k}
is the identity on
(I-P_{k})\mathfrak{X}
, and we recall that
P_{k}\mathfrak{X}=G_{k}
.

From Lemma
7.1
, the bound
\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\|\iota_{k}\|\leq 2
, and (
10.9
), it follows that

[\iota_{k}^{-1}\tilde{V}_{k}\iota_{k}]_{\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(G_{k}))}\leq\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\|\iota_{k}\|[\tilde{V}_{k}]_{\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(H_{k}))}\lesssim 1.

Then Lemma
7.2
shows that

(10.12)

[V_{k}]_{\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))}\approx[I_{k}]_{\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}((I-P_{k})\mathfrak{X}))}+[\iota_{k}^{-1}\tilde{V}_{k}\iota_{k}]_{\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(G_{k}))}\lesssim 1.

Noting that

V_{k}^{-*}:=I_{k}^{*}\oplus\iota_{k}^{*}\tilde{V}_{k}^{-*}\iota_{k}^{-*}\quad\text{with respect to}\quad\mathfrak{X}^{*}=(I-P_{k}^{*})\mathfrak{X}^{*}\oplus P_{k}^{*}\mathfrak{X}^{*},

we also deduce, for
J\in\{J_{k},J_{k}\pm 1\}
and
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}
, that

\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J,V_{k}^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})&\lesssim\|(I-P_{k}^{*})\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}+\|\iota_{k}^{*}\|\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(\tilde{V}_{k}^{-*})}(\iota_{k}^{-*}P_{k}^{*}\mathfrak{e}^{*})\\
&\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}+\|\iota_{k}^{*}\|\|\iota_{k}^{-*}P_{k}^{*}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{H_{k}}\qquad\text{by \eqref{eq:specVk}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\iota_{k}^{*}\|\|\iota_{k}^{-*}\|)\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}.\end{split}

Similarly,

\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,V_{k})}(\mathfrak{e})&\lesssim\|(I-P_{k})\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}+\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,\tilde{V}_{k})}(\iota_{k}P_{k}\mathfrak{e})\\
&\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}+\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\|\iota_{k}P_{k}\mathfrak{e}\|_{H_{k}}\qquad\text{by \eqref{eq:specVk}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\|\iota_{k}\|)\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}.\end{split}

Thus we have shown that

(10.13)

\begin{cases}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J,V_{k}^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}&\forall\ \mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*},\\
\quad\ \rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,V_{k})}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}&\forall\ \mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}\end{cases}

for
J\in\{J_{k},J_{k}\pm 1\}
. If
J=[h,h+1]
with
h\in\mathbb{Z}
in any other unit interval besides the previous three, then
V_{k}\equiv I
on
J
, so that both the left and the right-hand sides of (
10.13
) reduce to just
\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}
, and (
10.13
) holds trivially also in this case. Thus (
10.13
) is valid for all
J=[h,h+1]
,
h\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The combined weight
V

We now define a single weight
V
on
\mathbb{R}
by setting

(10.14)

V(x):=\begin{cases}V_{k}(x)&\text{if }x\in[4k-1,4k+2]\text{ for some }k\geq 1,\\
I&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that we actually have
V(x)=V_{k}(x)
if
x\in[4k-2,4k+3]
, since
V_{k}(x)=I
outside
[4k-1,4k+2]
. To show that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))
, we need to verify that

(10.15)

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J;V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J;V)}^{*}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})

for all finite intervals
J\subset\mathbb{R}
.

Suppose first that
|J|\leq 1
. With
m:=\lfloor\inf J\rfloor\in\mathbb{Z}
, it follows that
J\subset[m,m+2]
. Now
m\in 4k+\{-2,-1,0,1\}
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and hence
J\subset[m,m+2]\subset[4k-2,4k+3]
. Since
V\equiv V_{k}
on this interval, (
10.15
) follows from (
10.12
).

Hence it only remains to consider
|J|>1
. With
m:=\lfloor\inf J\rfloor
and
n:=\lceil\sup J\rceil
, we have
J\subseteq[m,n]=:\hat{J}
and
|\hat{J}|\leq 2+|J|\leq 3|J|
. Then

\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})&=\Big(\frac{1}{|J|}\int_{J}\|V^{-*}(\cdot)\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}^{p^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\\
&\leq\Big(\frac{3}{|\hat{J}|}\sum_{h=m}^{n-1}\int_{[h,h+1]}\|V^{-*}(\cdot)\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}^{p^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\\
&\lesssim\Big(\frac{1}{|\hat{J}|}\sum_{h=m}^{n-1}\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}^{p^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\qquad\text{by \eqref{eq:Vk=I}}\\
&=\Big(\frac{1}{|\hat{J}|}(n-m)\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}=\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}},\end{split}

where, in the application of (
10.13
), we observed that
V\equiv V_{k}
for some
k=k(h)
on each interval
[h,h+1]
, while (
10.13
) is valid for all pairs of
k
and
h
, as observed in the discussion following (
10.13
).

Similarly,

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}

by the other estimate in (
10.13
). The definition of the dual norm and combination with the previous estimate shows that

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(J,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})\lesssim\|\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(J,V)}^{*}(\mathfrak{e}^{*}),

which is the required estimate (
10.15
) for the large intervals
|J|>1
and hence
shows that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))
.

The unboundedness of
\mathcal{H}
on
L^{p}(V)

It remains to show that
\mathcal{H}
is not bounded on
L^{p}(V)
. For this purpose, we recall the functions
f_{k}\in L^{p}(J_{k},E_{k})
and
g_{k}\in L^{p^{\prime}}(J_{k},E_{k})
from (
10.6
). Let us then consider the
H_{k}=E_{k}\oplus E_{k}
-valued functions
(f_{k},0)
and
(0,g_{k})
, and finally

(10.16)

\begin{split}\tilde{f}_{k}&:=j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}(f_{k},0)\in L^{p}(J_{k};\mathfrak{X}),\\
\tilde{g}_{k}&:=P_{k}^{*}\iota_{k}^{*}(0,g_{k})\in L^{p^{\prime}}(J_{k};\mathfrak{X}^{*}),\end{split}

recalling that

•

P_{k}:\mathfrak{X}\to G_{k}
are uniformly bounded projections,

•

j_{k}:G_{k}\to\mathfrak{X}
is the injection
\mathfrak{e}\mapsto\mathfrak{e}
of
G_{k}\subseteq\mathfrak{X}
into
\mathfrak{X}
,

•

\iota_{k}:G_{k}\to H_{k}
are isomorphisms with
\|\iota_{k}\|\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\leq 2
; thus

•

\iota_{k}^{*}:H_{k}^{*}(=H_{k})\to G_{k}^{*}
are the adjoint isomorphisms,

•

P_{k}^{*}:G_{k}^{*}\to\mathfrak{X}^{*}
are uniformly bounded.

We also recall that, by (
10.14
) and (
10.11
), on the interval
J_{k}
, we have

V^{\pm 1}=V_{k}^{\pm 1}=(I-P_{k})+j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}\tilde{V}_{k}^{\pm 1}\iota_{k}P_{k}.

Hence

(10.17)

\begin{split}&\langle V\mathcal{H}V^{-1}\tilde{f}_{k},\tilde{g}_{k}\rangle\\
&\quad=\langle\iota_{k}P_{k}V_{k}\mathcal{H}V_{k}^{-1}j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}(f_{k},0),(0,g_{k})\rangle\\
&\quad=\langle\iota_{k}P_{k}j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}\tilde{V}_{k}\iota_{k}P_{k}\mathcal{H}j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}\tilde{V}_{k}^{-1}\iota_{k}P_{k}j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}(f_{k},0),(0,g_{k})\rangle\\
&\quad=\langle\tilde{V}_{k}\mathcal{H}\tilde{V}_{k}^{-1}(f_{k},0),(0,g_{k})\rangle,\end{split}

where we used
\iota_{k}P_{k}j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}=\iota_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}=I
and the fact that the constant operators
\iota_{k}P_{k}
or
j_{k}\iota_{k}^{-1}
commute with
\mathcal{H}
.

Next, from the definition of
\tilde{V}_{k}
in (
10.8
), we obtain

(10.18)

\begin{split}\langle\tilde{V}_{k}\mathcal{H}\tilde{V}_{k}^{-1}(f_{k},0),(0,g_{k})\rangle&=\Big\langle\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
\tilde{B}_{k}&I\end{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
-\tilde{B}_{k}&I\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}f_{k}\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
g_{k}\end{pmatrix}\Big\rangle\\
&=\Big\langle\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}&0\\
[\tilde{B}_{k},\mathcal{H}]&\mathcal{H}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}f_{k}\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
g_{k}\end{pmatrix}\Big\rangle\\
&=\langle[\tilde{B}_{k},\mathcal{H}]f_{k},g_{k}\rangle=\langle[B_{k},\mathcal{H}]f_{k},g_{k}\rangle,\end{split}

using in the last step the fact that
\tilde{B}_{k}=B_{k}
on
J_{k}
, since
\tilde{B}_{k}
was obtained from
\{\delta_{k,h}B_{h}\}_{h\in\mathbb{Z}}
by the algorithm (
9.6
).

A combination of (
10.17
), (
10.18
), and (
10.6
) shows that

|\langle V\mathcal{H}V^{-1}\tilde{f}_{k},\tilde{g}_{k}\rangle|\gtrsim\log N_{k}.

On the other hand, from (
10.16
) and the normalisations of
f_{k}
and
g_{k}
, we find that

\begin{split}\|\tilde{f}_{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathfrak{X})}\|\tilde{g}_{k}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R};\mathfrak{X}^{*})}&\leq\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R};E_{k})}\cdot\|P_{k}^{*}\|\|\iota_{k}^{*}\|\|g_{k}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R};E_{k})}\\
&=\|\iota_{k}^{-1}\|\|\iota_{k}\|\|P_{k}\|\leq 2C.\end{split}

The last two displays clearly show the unboundedness of
V\mathcal{H}V^{-1}
on
L^{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathfrak{X})
, and hence of
\mathcal{H}
on
L^{p}(V;\mathfrak{X})
. Since we already checked that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))
, the proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Theorem
10.1
.

If
\mathfrak{X}
is not a UMD space, the Hilbert transform is already unbounded on
L^{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathfrak{X})
, so it suffices to take
V\equiv I
, the identity operator. If
\mathfrak{X}
is
K
-convex, then the required weight is provided by Proposition
10.4
. Since every UMD space is
K
-convex (see
[
39
, Proposition 4.3.10]
), these two cases cover all Banach spaces.
∎

11.
Another example of unboundedness

In
[
11
, second half of page 22]
, the following estimate is proved for
matrix
weights
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}
(written as
V=W^{\frac{1}{p}}
in
[
11
]
):

(11.1)

\fint_{Q}\Big(\fint_{Q}\|V(x)f(y)\|\,dy\Big)^{p}\,dx\lesssim\fint_{Q}\|V(y)f(y)\|^{p}\,dy.

Indeed, after writing
V(x)f(y)=V(x)V(y)^{-1}V(y)f(y)
, the proof is an easy estimate using nothing but Hölder’s inequality and one of the equivalent definitions of
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}
for matrix weights. Nevertheless, the said equivalent condition is the one that does not extend to the operator-valued setting. Despite the apparent simplicity of (
11.1
), we will show the following:

Example 11.1
.

For every
p\in(1,\infty)
, estimate (
11.1
) fails, in general, for operator-valued weights
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\ell^{p}))
.

The example uses an idea from
[
46
, paragraph after Corollary 4.7]
.

Proof of Example
11.1
.

We consider a diagonal-valued weight
V(x)=\operatorname{diag}(v_{i}(x))_{i=1}^{\infty}
, where all
v_{i}\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R})
are scalar weights with uniformly bounded
\mathscr{A}_{p}
constants. Let
\mathfrak{e}=(\lambda_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}\in\ell^{p}
. Then

(11.2)

\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})^{p}&=\fint_{Q}\|V(x)\mathfrak{e}\|_{\ell^{p}}^{p}\,dx=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\fint_{Q}|v_{i}(x)|^{p}\,dx|\lambda_{i}|^{p}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Big(\fint_{Q}|v_{i}(x)|^{-p^{\prime}}\,dx\Big)^{-\frac{p}{p^{\prime}}}|\lambda_{i}|^{p}\quad\text{since $v_{i}\in\mathscr{A}_{p}$ uniformly}\\
&=\sup\Big\{\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Big(\fint_{Q}|v_{i}(x)|^{-p^{\prime}}\,dx\Big)^{-\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\lambda_{i}\mu_{i}\Big|^{p}:\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|\mu_{i}|^{p^{\prime}}\leq 1\Big\}\\
&=\sup\Big\{\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}\sigma_{i}\Big|^{p}:\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Big(\fint_{Q}|v_{i}(x)|^{-p^{\prime}}\,dx\Big)|\sigma_{i}|^{p^{\prime}}\leq 1\Big\}\\
&=\sup\Big\{|\langle\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|^{p}:\fint_{Q}\|V(x)^{-1}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\ell^{p^{\prime}}}^{p^{\prime}}\,dx\leq 1\Big\}=\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}^{*}(\mathfrak{e})^{p},\end{split}

introducing a dual vector
\mathfrak{e}^{*}=(\sigma_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
on the last line. (To make sure that
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\ell^{p^{\prime}}
, the suprema in the previous computation may be taken over finitely non-zero sequences of
\mu_{i}
and
\sigma_{i}
only.) Thus
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}
.

For our example, we will make a more concrete choice as follows. Recall that
w(x)=|x|^{\gamma}\in A_{p}(\mathbb{R})
if and only if
-1<\gamma<p-1
; hence
v(x)=|x|^{\beta}=w(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R})
if and only if
-\frac{1}{p}<\beta<\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
. If further
\phi(x)=|x|^{\alpha}
, then
\phi\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(v)
, i.e.,
v\phi\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})
if and only if
\alpha+\beta>-\frac{1}{p}
. For a small
\varepsilon>0
, we choose
\beta=\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon
and
\alpha=-1+2\varepsilon
.

For
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
k=0,\ldots,2^{j}-1
, we define

x_{2^{j}+k}:=2^{-j}k\in[0,1),\qquad\lambda_{2^{j}+k}^{(N)}:=2^{-j(\frac{1}{p}+\varepsilon)}\mathbf{1}_{[0,N]}(j),

and functions
v_{i}(x)=v(x-x_{i})=|x-x_{i}|^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon}
and

f_{i}(x)=\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)}(x)\phi(x-x_{i})=\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)}(x)|x-x_{i}|^{-1+2\varepsilon}.

We then wish to show the failure of (
11.1
) for
V(x)=\operatorname{diag}(v_{i}(x))_{i=1}^{\infty}
and
f^{(N)}(x)=(\lambda_{i}^{(N)}f_{i}(x))_{i=1}^{\infty}
in place of
f
, in the sense that there is no uniform estimate with respect to the truncation parameter
N
. Since
f_{i}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
for each
i
, the truncation parameter ensures that
f^{(N)}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R};\ell^{p})
is well defined.

We consider
Q=[0,1)
. Then

\begin{split}\operatorname{RHS}\eqref{eq:p22}&=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(\lambda_{i}^{(N)})^{p}\int_{0}^{1}|v_{i}(x)f_{i}(x)|^{p}\,dx\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(\lambda_{i}^{(N)})^{p}\int_{0}^{1}|x|^{(-\frac{1}{p}+\varepsilon)p}\,dx\\
&\approx\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(\lambda_{i}^{(N)})^{p}=\sum_{j=0}^{N}2^{j}\cdot 2^{-j(\frac{1}{p}+\varepsilon)p}=\sum_{j=0}^{N}2^{-j\varepsilon p}\approx 1,\end{split}

where we are allowing dependence of the implicit constants on both
p
and
\varepsilon
, but not on
N
.
Here, and thereafter,
\operatorname{RHS}
(
11.1
)
(resp.
\operatorname{LHS}
(
11.1
))
means the right-hand (resp. left-hand) side
of (
11.1
).

We then turn to the left-hand side of (
11.1
). For fixed
x,y\in[0,1)
, we consider

(11.3)

\begin{split}\|V(x)f^{(N)}(y)\|_{\ell^{p}}^{p}&=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(\lambda_{i}^{(N)}v_{i}(x)f_{i}(y))^{p}\\
&=\sum_{j=0}^{N}2^{-j(\frac{1}{p}+\varepsilon)p}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{j}-1}|x-2^{-j}k|^{(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon)p}|y-2^{-j}k|^{(-1+2\varepsilon)p}.\end{split}

For each
j\geq 3
, we consider two cases:

Case 1:
|x-y|\geq 2\cdot 2^{-j}
.
In this case, let
k
be such that
k2^{-j}
is closest to
y
. Hence
|y-k2^{-j}|\leq 2^{-j}
and

|x-k2^{-j}|\geq|x-y|-|y-k2^{-j}|\geq|x-y|-2^{-j}\geq\frac{1}{2}|x-y|.

Case 2:
|x-y|<2\cdot 2^{-j}
.
In this case, let
k
be such that
3\cdot 2^{-j}\leq|y-k2^{-j}|<4\cdot 2^{-j}
. Then

|x-k2^{-j}|\geq|y-k2^{-j}|-|x-y|\geq 3\cdot 2^{-j}-|x-y|>\Big(\frac{3}{2}-1\Big)|x-y|.

Thus, in each case, we find a
k
such that
|x-2^{-j}k|\gtrsim|x-y|
and
|y-2^{-j}k|\lesssim 2^{-j}
; hence

|x-2^{-j}k|^{(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon)p}|y-2^{-j}k|^{(-1+2\varepsilon)p}\gtrsim|x-y|^{(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon)p}2^{j(1-2\varepsilon)p}.

Obviously, this is also a lower bound for the whole sum over
k
.

Substituting this into (
11.3
), we find that

\begin{split}\|V(x)f^{(N)}(y)\|_{\ell^{p}}^{p}&\gtrsim\sum_{j=3}^{N}2^{-j(\frac{1}{p}+\varepsilon)p}|x-y|^{(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon)p}2^{j(1-2\varepsilon)p}\\
&=|x-y|^{p-1-\varepsilon p}\sum_{j=3}^{N}2^{j(p-1-3\varepsilon p)}\\
&\geq|x-y|^{p-1-\varepsilon p}2^{N(p-1-3\varepsilon p)}.\end{split}

Thus

\operatorname{RHS}\eqref{eq:p22}\gtrsim 2^{N(p-1-3\varepsilon p)}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}|x-y|^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-\varepsilon}\,dy\Big)^{p}\,dx\approx 2^{N(p-1-3\varepsilon p)}.

Choosing
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{3p^{\prime}})
, this contradicts (
11.1
) as
N\to\infty
, recalling that
\operatorname{LHS}\eqref{eq:p22}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
N
.
∎

Part III
Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces

12.
Main definitions

In this part, we develop the real-variable theory of
Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces associated with
the
\mathscr{A}_{p}
weights.
We begin with the following definition of
Littlewood–Paley functions.

Definition 12.1
.

Let
\sqrt{2}<\alpha<\beta<\pi
.
We say that
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is an
(\alpha,\beta)
-Littlewood–Paley function if

1_{[\frac{1}{\alpha},\alpha]}(|\xi|)\lesssim|\hat{\varphi}(\xi)|\lesssim 1_{[\frac{1}{\beta},\beta]}(|\xi|).

We say that
\varphi
is a Littlewood–Paley function if it is an
(\alpha,\beta)
-Littlewood–Paley function for some
\sqrt{2}<\alpha<\beta<\pi
.

For a Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we denote

\varphi_{j}(x):=2^{jn}\varphi(2^{j}x).

Most of the time, we could simply consider Littlewood–Paley functions with some fixed
(\alpha,\beta)
, say
(\frac{5}{3},2)
as in
[
10
, Eqs. (3.2) and (3.3)]
or
[
13
, Eq. (3.2)]
. The advantage of the slightly more general definition is the following simple observation:

Remark 12.2
.

Whenever
\varphi
exists a Littlewood–Paley function, there exists another Littlewood–Paley function
\chi
such that
\chi_{j}*\varphi_{j}=\varphi_{j}
. Indeed, if
\varphi
is an
(\alpha,\beta)
-Littlewood–Paley function, it suffices to take
\chi
to be a
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})
-Littlewood–Paley function for some
\beta^{\prime}\in(\beta,\pi)
with the additional property that
\hat{\chi}(\xi)=1
for
|\xi|\in[\frac{1}{\beta},\beta]
.

Similarly to
[
26
,
28
,
59
]
, we will also
introduced and study average-weighted spaces related to
a family of quasi-norms.
Let
\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
be a family of quasi-norms on a Banach space
\mathfrak{X}
.
For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define piecewise variable quasi-norms of a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathfrak{X}
by

\begin{cases}(\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}f)(x):=\rho_{Q}(f(x)),\\
\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\mathscr{D}_{j})}(x):=\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q)},\end{cases}\qquad x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}.

Recall that the
notation

\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
denotes
the set of all bounded linear functionals
f:\mathscr{S}_{\infty}\to\mathfrak{X}
.
Now we can define the following
weighted Besov/Triebel–Lizorkin norms by pointwise and averaging weights.

Definition 12.3
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and

s\in\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
V
is a weight,

\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
is a family of quasi-norms, and

A\in\{B,F\},\qquad(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}:=\begin{cases}L^{p}\ell^{q},&A=F,\\
\ell^{q}L^{p},&A=B.\end{cases}

(i)

Assume that
\varphi
is an
(\alpha,\beta)
-Littlewood–Paley function,

\sqrt{2}<\alpha<\beta<\pi
.
The
homogeneous operator-weighted
Besov space
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)
and the

homogeneous operator-weighted
Triebel–Lizorkin space
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)

are defined by setting

\displaystyle\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi):=\Big\{f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X}):\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)}<\infty\Big\},

where, for any
f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)}

\displaystyle:=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}}.

The
homogeneous averaging operator-weighted
Besov space
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\rho)
and the

homogeneous averaging operator-weighted
Triebel–Lizorkin space
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\rho)

are defined by setting

\displaystyle\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\varphi):=\Big\{f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X}):\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\varphi)}<\infty\Big\},

where, for any
f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\varphi)}

\displaystyle:=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}}.

(ii)

For any sequence

t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
of vectors
t_{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}
,
define

(12.1)

t_{j}:=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}t_{Q}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}},\quad j\in\mathbb{Z},

and

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|Vt_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}},\quad\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)}=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(t_{j})\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}}.

The
homogeneous operator-weighted
Besov sequence space
\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(V)
and the

homogeneous operator-weighted
Triebel–Lizorkin sequence space
\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(V)

are defined by setting

\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(V):=\Big\{t=(t_{Q}):\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}<\infty\Big\}.

The
homogeneous averaging operator-weighted
Besov sequence space
\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)
and the

homogeneous averaging operator-weighted
Triebel–Lizorkin sequence space
\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)

are defined by setting

\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho):=\Big\{t=(t_{Q}):\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)}<\infty\Big\}.

The main example of
\rho

is
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

and its relatives studied above,
but parts of the real-variable theory can be conveniently developed
in a more general framework (see Definition
15.1
below).

13.
Fourier analysis preliminaries

In this section, we establish some basic tools
in Fourier analysis, including the Calderón reproducing
formula and the sampling type inequality in infinite-dimensional
target spaces. They will play key roles in developing
the real-variable theory of operator-valued Besov
and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.

We begin with the following Calderón reproducing formula.

Lemma 13.1
.

Let
\sqrt{2}<\alpha<\beta<\pi
.
Let
\varphi
be an
(\alpha,\beta)
-Littlewood–Paley function, and let
\gamma\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be such that
\hat{\gamma}(\xi)=1
for
|\xi|\leq\beta
and
\operatorname{supp}\hat{\gamma}\subset\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|<\pi\}
. Then for all
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
and all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(13.1)

\varphi_{j}*f(x)=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}2^{-jn}\gamma_{j}(x-x_{R}-y)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x_{R}+y),

where
\gamma_{j}(x):=2^{jn}\gamma(2^{j}x)
.

Proof.

For scalar-valued
f
, this is
[
13
, Lemma 3.4]
, applied to the distribution
\varphi_{j}*f
with Fourier support in
2^{j}\operatorname{supp}\hat{\varphi}\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\leq\beta\cdot 2^{j}\}
. The general case is obtained either by repeating the same proof or by the following reduction to the scalar case: For
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
and
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}
, we have
\langle\mathfrak{e}^{*},f\rangle:=\mathfrak{e}^{*}\circ f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and hence (
13.1
) holds with
\langle\mathfrak{e}^{*},f\rangle
in place of
f
by the scalar-valued result
[
13
, Lemma 3.4]
. This may be written as

\langle\mathfrak{e}^{*},\varphi_{j}*f(x)\rangle=\Big\langle\mathfrak{e}^{*},\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}2^{-jn}\gamma_{j}(x-x_{R}-y)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x_{R}+y)\Big\rangle

for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
as in the statement and for all
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}
.
Since the dual elements
\mathfrak{e}^{*}
separate the points of
\mathfrak{X}
, this implies (
13.1
) as written.
∎

We now turn to the sampling type inequality.
Recall that a
seminorm
is like a norm except that it is allowed to have a nontrivial null space.
Moreover, we need the following concept of

u
-seminorms.

Definition 13.2
.

Let
u\in(0,1]
. A
u
-(semi)norm

\rho
is like a (semi)norm except that the triangle inequality is replaced by

\rho(\mathfrak{e}+\mathfrak{v})^{u}\leq\rho(\mathfrak{e})^{u}+\rho(\mathfrak{v})^{u}\quad\forall\,\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{v}\in\mathfrak{X}.

It easily follows that any
u
-(semi)norm is also
an
s
-(semi)norm for all
s\in(0,u)
.
In particular, any norm is also a
u
-norm for all
u\in(0,1)
.
Based on this, we now derive the following sampling type inequality.

Lemma 13.3
.

Let
\varphi
be a Littlewood–Paley function.
If
f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
and
\rho
is a
u
-seminorm on
\mathfrak{X}
, then
for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\rho(\varphi_{j}*f(x))^{u}\lesssim\big(\phi_{j}*\rho(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\big)(x),

where
\phi(x):=(1+|x|)^{-M}
with
M>0
taken as large as we like and where
the implicit positive constant is independent of
j
,
f
, and
x
.

Under the same assumptions, if
P\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
, we even have

\sup_{x\in P}\rho(\varphi_{j}*f(x))^{u}\lesssim\inf_{y\in P}\big(\phi_{j}*\rho(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\big)(y),

where the implicit positive constant is independent of
j
,
f
, and
P
.

Proof.

Let
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be arbitrary, and
y\in[0,2^{-j})^{n}
.
We apply
\rho(\cdot)^{u}
to both sides of (
13.1
) and use the
u
-triangle inequality to find that

\begin{split}\rho(\varphi_{j}*f(x))^{u}&\leq\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}(2^{-jn}|\gamma_{j}(x-x_{R}-y)|)^{u}\rho((\varphi_{j}*f)(x_{R}+y))^{u}.\end{split}

Averaging over
y\in[0,2^{-j})^{n}
and using the decay
|\gamma(x)|\lesssim(1+|x|)^{-M/u}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\rho(\varphi_{j}*f(x))^{u}&\lesssim\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}2^{jn}\int_{R}(1+2^{j}|x-z|)^{-M}\rho((\varphi_{j}*f)(z))^{u}\,dz\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}(x-z)\rho((\varphi_{j}*f)(z))^{u}\,dz=(\phi_{j}*\rho((\varphi_{j}*f))^{u})(x),\end{split}

which is the first claimed inequality.

If
x,y\in P\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then
\phi_{j}(x-z)\approx\phi_{j}(y-z)
, so that the right-hand side of the previous bound is comparable to
(\phi_{j}*\rho((\varphi_{j}*f))^{u})(y)
, and we also obtain the second claim.
∎

14.
Relations between pointwise and average-weighted spaces

In this section, we study the relationships between pointwise-weighted spaces and
average-weighted spaces with

\rho=\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)}=\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}.

Since we always work with the same Littlewood–Paley function

\varphi
, to simplify the presentation,
we omit the
\varphi
and denote

\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\cdots,\varphi)
simply as
\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\cdots)

throughout this section.

14.1.
Besov spaces

We first study this problem for Besov spaces.
For Besov sequence spaces, we have the following result.

Theorem 14.1
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
and
V
be a weight.

(i)

For any sequence
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}
,

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}=\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\mathscr{D},V)})}.

(ii)

Suppose
r\in(0,\infty)
. The equivalence

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\approx\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}

holds for all sequences
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
of vectors
in
\mathfrak{X}
if and only if, for all cubes
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and all

\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
,

(14.1)

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})

with the positive equivalence constants independent of
Q
and
\mathfrak{e}
.

Proof.

By a direct calculation, we obtain, for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\|Vt_{Q}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\frac{{\bf 1}_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int_{Q}\|V(x)t_{Q}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|^{-\frac{p}{2}}|Q|\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})^{p}=\Big\|\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})\frac{{\bf 1}_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

This, together with the definitions of
both sequence spaces [namely Definition
12.3
(
ii
)],
finishes the proof of (
i
).

On the other hand, by testing the sequence

t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
with

t_{Q}\neq{\bf 0}
only when
Q=R
for a given cube
R\in\mathscr{D}
,
we conclude that (
14.1
) holds for all
Q
and
\mathfrak{X}
if and only if

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\mathscr{D},V)})}\approx\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}.

Hence, from (
i
), it follows that
(
ii
) holds. This then finishes the proof of
Theorem
14.1
.
∎

Corollary 14.2
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
, then
(
14.1
) holds for all

Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
.
As a consequence, for any sequence
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}
,

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\approx\|t\|_{\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
t
.

Proof.

Since
V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\max\Big\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e}),\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{\textit{\L}^{\max\{p,r\}}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{by H\"{o}lder's inequality}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\Big\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e}),\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\Big\}\quad\text{by Corollary \ref {cor:Ap1}}.

This shows Corollary
14.2
.
∎

Now, we turn to investigate the relationship between
Besov spaces
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V)
and

\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})
.

Theorem 14.3
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
, then,
for any
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\approx\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.

Indeed, for each side of the above equivalence,
we can prove some more general results,
which are also of independent interest.
On the one hand,
the following result about dominating the pointwise weighted norm by the averaging weighted norm is inspired by
[
1
, Eq. (3.14)]
, where a similar estimate for operator-weighted Bergman spaces of analytic functions in the unit disc was obtained. Note that the components
\varphi_{j}*f
of the Littlewood–Paley decomposition, having compactly supported Fourier transforms, are also analytic. In the proof of
[
1
, Eq. (3.14)]
, the analyticity of
f
was used via the resulting subharmonicity of
z\mapsto\|W^{\frac{1}{2}}(\zeta)f(z)\|^{2}
, meaning that pointwise values of this function (in particular at
z=\zeta
) are dominated by its averages over discs. Our substitute for this is Lemma
13.3
, where the pointwise values of
\rho(\varphi*f)^{u}
are dominated by its convolution averages.

Proposition 14.4
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,

V
be a weight, and
\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

be a sequence of quasi-norms such that

(14.2)

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim\rho_{Q}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{for all}\ Q\in\mathscr{D}\ \text{and}\ \mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}

and

\rho
satisfies that

(14.3)

\frac{\rho_{Q}(\mathfrak{e})}{\rho_{R}(\mathfrak{e})}\lesssim\Big(1+\frac{|x_{Q}-x_{R}|}{\ell(Q)}\Big)^{N}

for some
N\geq 0
, all
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}\setminus\{0\}
, and all cubes
Q,R\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
of equal size
\ell(Q)=\ell(R)
,
where the implicit positive constants are independent of
\mathfrak{e}
,
Q
, and
R
.
Then for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
, and
y\in Q
, we have

(14.4)

\Big(\fint_{Q}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|^{p}\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\Big(\phi_{j}*\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}}(y),

where
\phi(x):=(1+|x|)^{-M}
,
M\in(0,\infty)
can be chosen as large as we like,
and
u\in(0,1]
can be chosen as small as we like. As a consequence,

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)},

where the implicit positive constant is independent of
f
.

Proof.

With
\rho(\mathfrak{e})=\|V(x)\mathfrak{e}\|
, Lemma
13.3
gives, for all

x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
,

\begin{split}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|&\lesssim\big(\phi_{j}^{\prime}*\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(\cdot)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u})^{\frac{1}{u}}(x_{Q})\\
&=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(x_{Q}-z)\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}\,dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}},\end{split}

where
u\in(0,1]
,
\phi^{\prime}(x):=(1+|x|)^{-M^{\prime}}
, and
M^{\prime}>0
may be chosen as large as we like.

Choosing
u\in(0,\min(p,1)]
, taking
\textit{\L}^{p}(Q)
norms of both sides, and using Minkowski’s inequality, we obtain

\begin{split}\Big(\fint_{Q}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}&\lesssim\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(x_{Q}-z)\Big[\fint_{Q}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx\Big]^{\frac{u}{p}}\,dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}}\\
&=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(x_{Q}-z)\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\varphi_{j}*f(z))^{u}\,dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}}\\
&\lesssim\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(x-z)\rho_{Q}(\varphi_{j}*f(z))^{u}\,dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}}\quad\text{by \eqref{prop:BV<Brhoe1}}.\end{split}

Since
\rho
satisfies (
14.3
), we deduce that

(14.5)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}&\phi_{j}^{\prime}(x-z)\rho_{Q}(\varphi_{j}*f(z))^{u}\,dz\\
&\lesssim\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\int_{R}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(x-z)(1+2^{j}|x_{Q}-x_{R}|)^{Nu}\rho_{R}(\varphi_{j}*f(z))^{u}\,dy\\
&\approx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}(y-z)\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi*f)(z)^{u}\,dz,\end{split}

where
\phi(z):=\phi^{\prime}(z)(1+|z|)^{Nu}=(1+|z|)^{-M}
and
M=M^{\prime}-Nu
can still be chosen as large as we like by taking
M^{\prime}
large enough. Thus we have proved (
14.4
).

To conclude the proof, we observe that

\begin{split}\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{L^{p}}^{p}&=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|\fint_{Q}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|^{p}\,dx\lesssim\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|\fint_{Q}\Big(\phi_{j}*\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\Big)^{\frac{p}{u}}(y)\,dy\\
&=\|\phi_{j}*\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\|_{L^{\frac{p}{u}}}^{\frac{p}{u}}\leq\Big(\|\phi_{j}\|_{L^{1}}\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\|_{L^{\frac{p}{u}}}\Big)^{\frac{p}{u}}\lesssim\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.\end{split}

Taking the
p
-th roots, multiplying by
2^{js}
and taking the
\ell^{q}
norms over
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we obtain the claim of the proposition.
∎

On the other hand, the following converse to Proposition
14.4
is inspired by the second part of
[
1
, Theorem 3.1]
and the key ingredient in
[
1
, Lemma 3.2]
. The idea borrowed from there is the following: In order to estimate
\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})
, in the presence of the
\mathscr{A}_{p}
condition, it suffices to estimate the dual norm
\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}^{*}(\mathfrak{e})
. Estimating the latter, by definition, is about estimating the pairings
\langle\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle=\langle V(\cdot)\mathfrak{e},V(\cdot)^{-*}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle
, where we can introduce the pointwise weight as just indicated. The remaining technical details of the argument differ from
[
1
]
, but this basic idea is the same. The estimation of the dual norms is handled in the following lemma, after which the proof of Theorem
14.3
is relatively easy.

Lemma 14.5
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
and
V
be a
p
-weight.
If
V^{-*}
is a
p^{\prime}
-weight and
V
satisfies, for some
N\geq 0
,

(14.6)

\rho_{L^{p^{\prime}}(R,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})\lesssim\Big(1+\frac{|x_{Q}-x_{R}|}{\ell(Q)}\Big)^{N}\rho_{L^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})

for all
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}
and all cubes
Q,R
of equal size
with the implicit positive constant independent of
\mathfrak{e}^{*}
,
Q
, and
R
,
then, for all
f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, and

x,y\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
,

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}^{*}(\varphi_{j}*f(x))\lesssim(\phi_{j}*\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|^{u})^{\frac{1}{u}}(y)

with the implicit positive constant independent
of
j
,
Q
, and
f
,
where
\phi_{j}(z)=2^{jn}\phi(2^{j}z)
with
\phi(z)=(1+|z|)^{-M}

and
M>0
can be chosen as large as we like, while
u>0
can be chosen as small as we like.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
13.3
with the seminorm
\rho(\mathfrak{e})=|\langle\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|
for a fixed
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}
. For
x,y\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
, this gives

\begin{split}|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(x),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|&\lesssim\big(\phi_{j}^{\prime}*|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(\cdot),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}(y)=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y-z)|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(z),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|^{r}\,dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{r}},\end{split}

where
r\in(0,1]
,
\phi^{\prime}(x):=(1+|x|)^{-M^{\prime}}
, and
M^{\prime}>0
may be chosen as large as we like.

Next, we note that

\begin{split}|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(z),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|&=|\langle V(z)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z),V(z)^{-*}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|\leq\|V(z)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\|V(z)^{-*}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}.\end{split}

Hence

|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(x),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|\lesssim\Big(\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y-x_{R})|R|\fint_{R}\|V(z)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{r}\|V(z)^{-*}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}^{r}\,dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

Let
\frac{1}{u}:=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
and
\frac{1}{v}:=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}
, recalling that
p^{\prime}:=\infty
for
p\in(0,1]
.
Since
r>0
can be chosen as small as we like, hence
\frac{1}{r}
as large as we like, we see that both
\frac{1}{u}
and
\frac{1}{v}
can be chosen as large as we like, and hence both
u,v>0
as small as we like.
Then

\begin{split}&\fint_{R}\|V(z)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{r}\|V(z)^{-*}\mathfrak{e}^{*}\|_{\mathfrak{X}^{*}}^{r}\,dz\\
&\quad\leq\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\textit{\L}^{u}(R;\mathfrak{X})}^{r}\big(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(R,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})\big)^{r}\\
&\quad\lesssim\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\textit{\L}^{u}(R;\mathfrak{X})}^{r}\big(1+2^{j}|x_{Q}-x_{R}|)^{Nr}\big(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})\big)^{r}.\end{split}

Choosing
M^{\prime}:=M+Nr
, we obtain

\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y-x_{R})\big(1+2^{j}|x_{Q}-x_{R}|\big)^{Nr}\approx\phi_{j}(y-x_{R})\approx\phi_{j}(y-x),

where
\phi(z):=(1+|z|)^{-M}
.
Substituting back, we have

\begin{split}\frac{|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(x),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|}{\rho_{L^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})}&\lesssim\Big(\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\phi_{j}(y-x_{R})|R|\cdot\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\textit{\L}^{u}(R;\mathfrak{X})}^{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{r}}\\
&\leq\Big(\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\phi_{j}(y-x_{R})|R|\cdot\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\textit{\L}^{u}(R;\mathfrak{X})}^{u}\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}}\Big(\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\phi_{j}(y-x_{R})|R|\Big)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{u}}\\
&=:I\times II,\\
\end{split}

where
II\approx\|\phi_{j}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{u}}\approx 1
, and

I^{u}=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\phi_{j}(y-x_{R})\int_{R}\|V(z)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|^{u}\,dz\approx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{j}(y-z)\|V(z)(\varphi_{j}*f)(z)\|^{u}\,dz.

Hence

\frac{|\langle\varphi_{j}*f(x),\mathfrak{e}^{*}\rangle|}{\rho_{L^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}(\mathfrak{e}^{*})}\lesssim(\phi_{j}*\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|^{u})^{\frac{1}{u}}(y).

Taking the supremum over
\mathfrak{e}^{*}\in\mathfrak{X}^{*}\setminus\{0\}
and using the definition of the dual norm, we obtain

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}^{*}(\varphi_{j}*f(x))\\
\lesssim(\phi_{j}*\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|^{u})^{\frac{1}{u}}(y),

which completes the proof of Lemma
14.5
.
∎

We now show the converse of Proposition
14.4
.
Indeed, we can prove this for both Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces in a unified way.

Proposition 14.6
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
, then,
for any
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(V)},

where the implicit positive constant is independent of
f
.

Proof.

Let
w:=\max\{p,r\}
and fix
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By the proof of Corollary
14.2
, we
find that the assumption
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}
implies

(14.7)

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})

for all
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
.
From Corollary
4.10
for
w>1
and Proposition
4.8
(
iii
) for
w\in(0,1]
, we then conclude that, for a.e.
x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
,

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\varphi_{j}\ast f(x))\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\varphi_{j}*f(x))\lesssim\begin{cases}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}^{*}(\varphi_{j}*f(x)),&w>1,\\
\displaystyle\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}},&w\leq 1.\end{cases}

Hence, for the case
w\leq 1
,
we have, for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f(x))\lesssim\|V(x)\varphi_{j}\ast f(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}.

The claim of the proposition in this case then follows by multiplying both sides by
2^{js}
and taking the
(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}
norms.

On the other hand,
we would like to apply Lemma
14.5
to deal with the case
w>1
.
By Corollary
6.9
, the required assumption (
14.6
), with

p=w
and
N=n
, follows from the present assumption that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}
.
Thus the said Lemma applies to show that, for any
x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
,

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w^{\prime}}(Q,V^{-*})}^{*}(\varphi_{j}*f(x))\lesssim(\phi_{j}*\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|^{u})^{\frac{1}{u}}(x).

This further implies that

\begin{split}\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V))}}&\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{jsu}\phi_{j}*\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|^{u}\Big\}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{p}{u}}\ell^{\frac{q}{u}})_{A}}^{\frac{1}{u}}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{jsu}\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|^{u}\Big\}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{p}{u}}\ell^{\frac{q}{u}})_{A}}^{\frac{1}{u}}=\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(V)},\end{split}

where the second inequality holds provided that both
\frac{p}{u}\in(1,\infty)
and
\frac{q}{u}\in(1,\infty]
. Recalling that
u>0
in Lemma
14.5
can be chosen arbitrarily small, we can clearly satisfy the said requirement. Fixing such a choice, the proof is complete.
∎

The proof of Theorem
14.3
is now a matter of collecting the pieces:

Proof of Theorem
14.3
.

The “
\gtrsim
” part is simply the Besov case of Proposition
14.6
.

To prove the “
\lesssim
” part of the present theorem,
we only need to check all the assumptions of
Proposition
14.4
with
\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

therein replaced by
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
.

Noting that the assumptions of Proposition
14.6
are the same as those of Theorem
14.3
, we can use (
14.7
), which implies both (
14.2
) and, in combination with Lemma
6.7
, also (
14.3
).

Thus the assumptions, and hence the conclusions, of Proposition
14.4
are satisfied, which completes the proof of the “
\lesssim
” part holds.
∎

14.2.
Triebel–Lizorkin spaces

In this subsection,
we extend Theorem
14.3

to Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.
We still cannot do this for whole range
q\in(0,\infty]
.
Indeed, for
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}
with
w>0
,
we need the following

optimal reverse Hölder lifting index

\varepsilon_{V}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon_{V}:=\sup\big\{\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)&:\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w+\varepsilon}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\\
&\qquad\text{holds for all cubes $Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}$ and
all $\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}$}\big\}.\end{split}

Note that, by Proposition
5.1
, the assumption that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}
always guarantees

\varepsilon_{V}>0
. Then we have the following equivalences for
Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.

Theorem 14.7
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,

V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
with optimal reverse Hölder lifting index

\varepsilon_{V}>0
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
q\in(0,\max\{p,r\}+\varepsilon_{V})
, then, for any

f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,

(14.8)

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\approx\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}

and, for any sequence
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}
,

(14.9)

\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\approx\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
and
t
.

Remark 14.8
.

If, in Theorem
14.7
,
let
p=r
and
\mathfrak{X}=\mathbb{C}^{m}
, then
the equivalences (
14.8
)
and (
14.9
)
hold for all
q\in(0,\infty]
, which were proved in

[
28
, Theorem 3.1]
.
However, when
\mathfrak{X}
is infinite-dimensional,
we only show these under the restriction

q\in(0,r+\varepsilon_{V})

in Theorem
14.7
. When
q=\infty
, we prove that the equivalence
(
14.9
) may not be true for general infinite-dimensional
case in Proposition
14.13
.
It is still unclear whether Theorem
14.7
holds
for the remaining case
q\in[r+\varepsilon_{V},\infty)
.

We first consider the “
\gtrsim
” part of Theorem
14.7
.
Indeed, this has already been done for (
14.8
) in Proposition
14.6
.
Hence, we now deal with the “
\gtrsim
” part of (
14.9
) as follows.

Proposition 14.9
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
, then

(14.10)

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}.

Proof.

Let
t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
and
w:=\max\{p,r\}
.
If
w\in(0,1]
, then,
for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
almost every
x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
, we have

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})\leq\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})\lesssim\|V(x)t_{Q}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}

by Proposition
4.8
(
ii
). Hence
\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(t_{j}(\cdot))\lesssim\|V(\cdot)t_{j}(\cdot)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}
, which implies (
14.10
) when
w\in(0,1]
.

Next, we consider
w\in(1,\infty)
.
Note that, for any given
u<\min\{p,q\}
and any
Q\in\mathscr{D}
,

(14.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})&\leq\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})\quad\text{by H\"{o}lder's inequality}\\
&\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{u}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})\quad\text{by Corollary \ref {cor:Ap1}}.\end{split}

Since
t_{j}
is a constant on every
Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
, that is,

t_{j}(x)=\frac{t_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}
for all
x\in Q
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{u}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\fint_{Q}\Big\|V(y)\frac{t_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}\,dy\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{u}}=\Big(\fint_{Q}\|V(y)t_{j}(y)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}\,dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{x\in Q}M_{u}(\|Vt_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{X}})(x),

where

M_{u}g(z):=\sup_{Q\owns z}\|g\|_{\textit{\L}^{u}(Q)}
is the rescaled maximal operator.

Using this, we further obtain, for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(t_{j})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{u}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})\frac{{\bf 1}_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\quad\text{by \eqref{f-arho<ave2}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{u}(\|Vt_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}).

Hence, from
u<\min\{p,q\}
and the Fefferman–Stein
vector-valued maximal inequality, we infer that

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(t_{j})\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}M_{u}(\|Vt_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{X}})\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|Vt_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}=\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(V)},

which shows (
14.10
) when
p\in[1,\infty)
.
Hence, we complete the proof of Proposition
14.9
.
∎

Next, we deal with the “
\lesssim
” part of Theorem
14.7
as follows.

Proposition 14.10
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,

V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}
with the optimal reverse Hölder lifting index

\varepsilon_{V}>0
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
q\in(0,\max\{p,r\}+\varepsilon_{V})
, then, for any

f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
,

(14.12)

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}

and, for any sequence
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}
,

(14.13)

\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})},

where the implicit positive constants are independent of
f
and
t
.

Proof.

We first show (
14.12
).
Fix
u\in(0,\min\{p,q\})
.
Then
\frac{p}{u},\frac{q}{u}\in(1,\infty)
. By the duality

(L^{\frac{p}{u}}\ell^{\frac{q}{u}})^{\prime}=L^{(\frac{p}{u})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{q}{u})^{\prime}}
,
we obtain

\begin{split}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}&=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}=\Big\|\Big\{2^{jsu}\|V(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{p}{u}}\ell^{\frac{q}{u}}}^{\frac{1}{u}}\\
&=\sup\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{jsu}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}g_{j}(x)\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{u}},\end{split}

where the supremum is taken over
\|\{g_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{(\frac{p}{u})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{q}{u})^{\prime}}}\leq 1
.

Define
w:=\max\{p,r\}
and choose
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{V})
such that,
for any
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
,

(14.14)

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w+\varepsilon}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e}).

For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}g_{j}(x)\,dx=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|\fint_{Q}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}g_{j}(x)\,dx,

where

\begin{split}\fint_{Q}&\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{u}g_{j}(x)\,dx\\
&\leq\Big(\fint_{Q}\|V(x)(\varphi_{j}*f)(x)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{w+\varepsilon}\,dx\Big)^{\frac{u}{w+\varepsilon}}\Big(\fint_{Q}g_{j}(x)^{(\frac{w+\varepsilon}{u})^{\prime}}\,dx\Big)^{1-\frac{u}{w+\varepsilon}}=:I^{u}\times II.\end{split}

Denoting by
M_{s}g(z):=\sup_{Q\owns z}\|g\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(Q)}
the
rescaled maximal function
, it holds

II\leq\inf_{y\in Q}M_{(\frac{w+\varepsilon}{u})^{\prime}}g(y).

To the factor
I
, we wish to apply Proposition
14.4
and more precisely its conclusion (
14.4
), but with
w+\varepsilon
and
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

in place of
p
and
\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
,
respectively. To this end, we need to verify the assumptions
(
14.2
) and (
14.3
), similarly modified.
Indeed, from
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}
, we infer that,
for every
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w+\varepsilon}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{by \eqref{eq:RHIepse1}}

\displaystyle\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{by \eqref{thm:equiB-e1}}

and, for every
R\in\mathscr{D}
such that
\ell(R)=\ell(Q)
,

\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})&\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{by \eqref{thm:equiB-e1}}\\
&\lesssim\Big(1+\frac{|x_{Q}-x_{R}|}{\ell(Q)}\Big)^{n}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(R,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{by Lemma \ref {lem:QvsR}}\\
&\approx\Big(1+\frac{|x_{Q}-x_{R}|}{\ell(Q)}\Big)^{n}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\quad\text{by \eqref{thm:equiB-e1}}.\end{split}

This shows that the assumptions (
14.2
) and (
14.3
)
are satisfied with
p
and
\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
replaced,
respectively, by
w+\varepsilon
and
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
.
Thus, applying (
14.4
), we obtain, for all
y\in Q
,

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\Big(\phi_{j}*\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{v}\Big)^{\frac{1}{v}}(y),\end{split}

where
v
can be chosen such that
v<\min\{p,q\}
.
Substituting back, it follows that

\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|\,I^{u}\times II\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\phi_{j}*\rho_{L^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{v}\Big)^{\frac{u}{v}}(y)\times M_{(\frac{w+\varepsilon}{u})^{\prime}}g_{j}(y)\,dy,

and hence

\begin{split}&\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{jsu}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}|Q|\,I^{u}\times II\\
&\quad\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{jsu}\Big(\phi_{j}*\rho_{L^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{v}\Big)^{\frac{u}{v}}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{p}{u}}\ell^{\frac{q}{u}}}\Big\|\Big\{M_{(\frac{w+\varepsilon}{u})^{\prime}}g_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{(\frac{p}{u})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{q}{u})^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad=:{\rm A}\cdot\Big\|\Big\{M_{(\frac{w+\varepsilon}{u})^{\prime}}g_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{(\frac{p}{u})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{q}{u})^{\prime}}}\lesssim{\rm A}\cdot\|\{g_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{(\frac{p}{u})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{q}{u})^{\prime}}}\leq{\rm A}\end{split}

by the Fefferman–Stein vector-valued maximal inequality,
since
(\frac{w+\varepsilon}{u})^{\prime}<\min\{(\frac{p}{u})^{\prime},(\frac{q}{u})^{\prime}\}

or equivalently
w+\varepsilon>\max\{p,q\}
.

Moreover, by the boundedness of
\{f_{j}\}\mapsto\{\phi_{j}*f_{j}\}

(note that
|\phi_{j}*f_{j}|\leq Mf_{j}
) on
L^{\frac{p}{v}}\ell^{\frac{q}{v}}

with
\frac{p}{v},\frac{q}{v}\in(1,\infty)
,

\begin{split}{\rm A}&=\Big\|\Big\{2^{jsv}\phi_{j}*\rho_{L^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{v}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{p}{v}}\ell^{\frac{q}{v}}}^{\frac{u}{v}}\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{jsv}\rho_{L^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{v}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{p}{v}}\ell^{\frac{q}{v}}}^{\frac{u}{v}}\\
&=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\rho_{L^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(\varphi_{j}*f)\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{u}=\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{L^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}^{u}.\end{split}

Combining everything, we have proved (
14.12
).

The proof of (
14.13
) is almost the same as
above. The only difference is that, for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the function
t_{j}
is constant on every
Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
(more precisely,

t_{j}(x)=\frac{t_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}
for all
x\in Q
).
Therefore, we have, for any
x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\fint_{Q}\|V(y)t_{j}(y)\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{w+\varepsilon}\,dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{w+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w+\varepsilon}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\quad\text{by \eqref{eq:RHIepse1}}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(t_{Q})}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\quad\text{by $V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}$ and \eqref{thm:equiB-e1}}

\displaystyle=\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(t_{j}(x)).

Using this to replace the above estimation for
I
, we can
obtain (
14.13
).
Hence, we complete the proof of Proposition
14.10
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
14.7
.

By the proofs of Theorem
14.3
and
Proposition
14.9
, we prove the “
\gtrsim
” part;
from Proposition
14.10
, we deduce
the “
\lesssim
” part.
Thus, we complete the proof of Theorem
14.7
.
∎

Finally, we turn to show that (
14.9
)
may not be true
q=\infty
. We first
show the following equivalent characterization
of the pointwise quasi-norm being dominated by the average quasi-norm,
which is of independent interest.

Proposition 14.11
.

Let
p,r\in(0,\infty)
,
V
be a weight, and

s\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then

(14.15)

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,\infty}(V)}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}

holds for all sequence
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}

with the implicit positive constant independent of
f

if and only if

(14.16)

\displaystyle\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|V(x)t_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx\lesssim\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R,V)}(t_{R})^{p}\,dx

holds for all
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\{t_{R}\}_{R\subset Q}

in
\mathfrak{X}

with the implicit positive constant independent of
Q
and
t
.

Remark 14.12
.

Concerning (
14.16
),
there are two remarks.

(i)

Suppose
\mathfrak{X}=\mathbb{C}^{m}
and
r=p
.
Let
\{A_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
denote the reducing operators of
V
(see, for instance,
[
28
, p. 490]
);
that is,
\{A_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
is a family of positive
m\times m
matrices such that,
for any
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
,

\rho_{\textit{\L}^{p}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\approx\|A_{Q}\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}

with the positive equivalence constants independent of
Q
and
\mathfrak{e}
.
Then it is easy to show that (
14.16
) is
equivalent to

(14.17)

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|V(x)A_{Q}^{-1}\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X})}^{p}\,dx<\infty,

which is
[
28
, (3.3)]
with
r=p
.
Hence, Proposition
14.13
below
indeed proves that, in the infinite-dimensional case,

[
28
, (3.3)]
may not be true.

(ii)

Note that (
14.17
) and its improvement
play important roles in the study
of the boundedness of Calderón–Zygmund operators
in the matrix-weighted case (see
[
35
]
).
Therefore, the failure of this key estimate
reveals another essential difficulty in
the operator-weighted theory.

Proof of Proposition
14.11
.

(
14.16
) follows immediately from (
14.15
).
Next, we consider the converse direction
(
14.16
)
\Rightarrow
(
14.15
).
By monotone convergence, to prove (
14.15
), we only
need to show, for any given
M\in\mathbb{N}
,

(14.18)

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|Vt_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\Big\}_{j\geq-M}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\infty}}^{p}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{f}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}^{p}.

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{LHS}\eqref{prop:fequi-e3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{j\geq-N}\Big|2^{js}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\|V(x)t_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\frac{{\bf 1}_{R}(x)}{|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:\ell(R)\leq 2^{M},x\in R}\ell(R)^{-(s+\frac{n}{2})p}\|V(x)t_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{-M}}\int_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:\ell(R)\leq 2^{M},x\in R}\ell(R)^{-(s+\frac{n}{2})p}\|V(x)t_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{-M}}\int_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:\ell(R)\leq 2^{M},x\in R}\ell(R)^{-(s+\frac{n}{2})p}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R,V)}(t_{R})^{p}\,dx\quad\text{by \eqref{prop:fequi-e2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{j\geq-N}\Big|2^{js}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R,V)}(t_{R})\frac{{\bf 1}_{R}(x)}{|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D}_{j},V)}(t_{j})\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\infty}}^{p}=\operatorname{RHS}\eqref{prop:fequi-e3}.

This implies (
14.16
)
\Rightarrow
(
14.15
)
and hence finishes the proof of Proposition
14.11
.
∎

Proposition 14.13
.

If
p\in(0,\infty)
and
r\in[1,\infty)
such that

\max\{p,r\}>1
and if
\mathfrak{X}:=\ell^{u}(\mathbb{Z})
for
u\in[1,\infty)
,
then there exists a weight
V\in\mathscr{A}_{\max\{p,r\}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))

such that (
14.15
) does not hold.

Proof.

Define
w:=\max\{p,r\}
.
From Proposition
14.11
,
we deduce that it is sufficient to find
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))

such that (
14.16
) does not hold.
Denote by
\mathfrak{X}_{1}:=\ell^{u}(\mathbb{N})
and

\mathfrak{X}_{2}:=\ell^{u}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathbb{N})
.
Then
\mathfrak{X}=\mathfrak{X}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{X}_{2}
.
For any given
B\in L^{w}_{\mathrm{loc},\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{2}))
,
define

\displaystyle V:=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
B&I\end{pmatrix}.

By Lemma
10.2
, we find that, if

B\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{2}))

and
B^{*}\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{2}^{*},\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{*}))
,
then
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}))
.
For given
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\{s_{R}\}_{R\subset Q}
in
\mathfrak{X}_{1}
,
define

t_{R}:=\begin{pmatrix}s_{R}\\
-\langle B\rangle_{R}s_{R}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{X}\quad\forall\,R\subset Q.

Then we have, for any
R\subset Q
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

(14.19)

\displaystyle V(x)t_{R}=\begin{pmatrix}s_{R}\\
(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\end{pmatrix}

and hence, if
B\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{2}))
,

(14.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R,V)}(t_{R})^{p}&=\Bigg\{\fint_{R}\Big[\|s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{u}+\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}^{u}\Big]^{\frac{r}{u}}\,dx\Bigg\}^{\frac{p}{r}}\\
&\approx\Bigg\{\fint_{R}\Big[\|s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{r}+\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}^{r}\Big]\,dx\Bigg\}^{\frac{p}{r}}\\
&\lesssim\|B\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{2}))}^{p}\|s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{p}\quad\text{by the John--Nirenberg inequality}.\end{split}

Using this, we further obtain, if (
14.16
) holds,
then, for all
\{s_{R}\}_{R\subset Q}

such that
\|s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}=1

\forall\,R\subset Q
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{2}}^{p}\,dx\\
&\quad\leq\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|V(x)t_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}}^{p}\,dx\quad\text{by (\ref {prop:fe1})}\\
&\quad\lesssim\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R,V)}(t_{R})^{p}\,dx\quad\text{by (\ref {prop:fequi-e2})}\\
&\quad\lesssim\|B\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{2}))}^{p}\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{p}\,dx\quad\text{by \eqref{prop:fe2}}\\
&\quad=\|B\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{2}))}^{p}.\end{split}

Since
\mathfrak{X}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{2}
are isometrically isomorphic, the above
argument implies that, to disprove (
14.16
),
we only need to find
B\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1}))

satisfying
B^{*}\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{*}))
, but
there exist a cube
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and a unit sequence
\{s_{R}\}_{R\subset Q}

in
\mathfrak{X}_{1}
such that

(14.21)

\displaystyle\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{p}\,dx\lesssim\|B\|_{\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1}))}^{p}

does not hold. We do this by borrowing some ideas from
[
58
, Remark 5.2]
.
For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
k=0,\ldots,2^{j}-1
, define

\displaystyle b_{2^{j}+k}(\cdot):=\log|x-2^{-j}k|\quad\text{and}\quad B:=\operatorname{diag}(b_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}.

Note that
b_{i}\in\operatorname{BMO}(\mathbb{R})
with a uniform norm with respect to
i
.
Thus, for any
\mathfrak{e}:=\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\in\mathfrak{X}_{1}
and any cube
Q
,
it holds that

\displaystyle\fint_{Q}\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{Q})\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{u}\,dx=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|e_{i}|^{u}\fint_{Q}|b_{i}(x)-\langle b_{i}\rangle_{Q}|^{u}\,dx\lesssim\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\|b_{i}\|_{\operatorname{BMO}(\mathbb{R})}^{u}\|\mathfrak{e}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{u}.

This, together with the John–Nirenberg inequality,
shows
B\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1}))
.
Similarly,
B^{*}\in\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{so}}(\mathbb{R};\mathscr{L}(\mathfrak{X}_{1}^{*}))
.
Now, let
Q:=[0,1)
and, if
R=Q_{j,k}:=2^{-j}[k,k+1)
for some

j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
k=0,\ldots,2^{j}-1
,
let
s_{R}:=\mathfrak{e}_{2^{j}+k}

(that is, the vector whose
(2^{j}+k)
-th element is 1 and all the other elements are 0).
Hence

(14.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{p}\,dx\\
&\quad\geq\int_{0}^{1}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},k=0,\ldots 2^{j}-1:x\in Q_{j,k}}|b_{2^{j}+k}(x)-\langle b_{2^{j}+k}\rangle_{Q_{j,k}}|^{p}\,dx.\end{split}

Observe that

\displaystyle b_{2^{j}+k}(x)-\langle b_{2^{j}+k}\rangle_{Q_{j,k}}=\log|x-2^{-j}k|+j\log 2+1=\log|2^{j}x-k|+1.

Note that
x\in Q_{j,k}
if and only if
2^{j}\in[k,k+1)
, in which case
2^{j}-k=\{2^{j}x\}\in[0,1)
is the fractional part of
2^{j}x
.
By this and (
14.22
), we conclude that

(14.23)

\displaystyle\fint_{Q}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}:x\in R\subset Q}\|(B(x)-\langle B\rangle_{R})s_{R}\|_{\mathfrak{X}_{1}}^{p}\,dx\geq\int_{0}^{1}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|\log\{2^{j}x\}+1|^{p}\,dx.

We now claim that

\inf_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\{2^{j}x\}=0
for almost every
x\in[0,1)
.
Indeed, if this claim holds, then the supremum on the right of (
14.23
) is
\infty
for a.e.
x
, thus the integral diverges, which disproves (
14.21
).

To check this claim, consider the binary expansion
x=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{a_{k}}{2^{k}}
with
a_{k}\in\{0,1\}
.
Hence
\{2^{j}x\}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{a_{k+j}}{2^{k}}
for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and we see that
\inf_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\{2^{j}x\}=0
if and only if
the binary expansion
(a_{k})_{k=1}^{\infty}
of
x
contains arbitrarily long strings of consecutive zeros. This is a special case of the property that
x
is
normal in base
2
; the fact that a.e.
x\in[0,1)
has this property is a classical result of Borel
[
7
, §11–12]
.
Therefore, we disprove (
14.21
)
and hence (
14.15
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
14.13
.
∎

15.
The
\varphi
transform of Frazier and Jawerth

For given Littlewood–Paley functions
\varphi
and
\psi
, Frazier and Jawerth
[
25
]
introduced the following of transformations, mapping distributions
f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to discrete sequences
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
of numbers indexed by the dyadic cubes, and vice versa. The same formulas make perfect sense for Banach space valued distributions
f\in\mathscr{S}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
and sequences
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}
:

(15.1)

S_{\varphi}f:=\{\langle f,\varphi_{Q}\rangle\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}},\qquad T_{\psi}t=T_{\psi}\big(\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}\big):=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}t_{Q}\psi_{Q},

where, for any
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{Q}(x):=|Q|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi\Big(\frac{x-x_{Q}}{\ell(Q)}\Big),\qquad\psi_{Q}(x):=|Q|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi\Big(\frac{x-x_{Q}}{\ell(Q)}\Big).\qquad

The goal of this section is to use the transforms (
15.1
) to establish a correspondence between the function space and the sequence space version
of the Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces
introduced in Definition
12.3
.
We first consider the average-weighted spaces.
Indeed, we can prove a
\varphi
-transform characterization and
many other properties of
\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)
under the following mild assumptions on
\rho
.

Definition 15.1
.

Let
u\in(0,1]
and
a,b,c\in[0,\infty)
.
A family
\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
of quasi-norms
is called a
family of
(u,a,b,c)
-norms
if

(i)

for each
Q\in\mathscr{D}
,
\rho_{Q}
is a
u
-norm
in the sense of Definition
13.2
,

(ii)

for each
Q\in\mathscr{D}
,
\rho_{Q}
is equivalent to
\|\cdot\|_{\mathfrak{X}}
,
where the positive equivalence constants may depend on
Q
,

(iii)

for all
Q,R\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
,

(15.2)

\rho_{Q}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim B_{a,b,c}(Q,R)\rho_{R}(\mathfrak{e}),

where
B(a,b,c)
is as in (
6.6
).

Remark 15.2
.

Let
r\in(0,\infty)
and
V
be a weight.
Under the following assumptions,
the family of quasi-norms
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

is a family of
(u,a,b,c)
-norms.

(i)

If
V
is an
(r,\beta)
-doubling weight
for
\beta\in[n,\infty)
in the sense of
Definition
6.2
, then, combining
Lemmas
2.3
and
6.6
, we conclude that

\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

is a family of
(\min\{1,r\},\frac{n}{r},\frac{\beta-n}{r},\frac{\beta}{r})
-norms.

(ii)

If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{w}
with
w\geq r
has doubling
dimension
\beta\in[n,\infty)
, then
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

is a family of
(\min\{1,r\},\frac{n}{w},\frac{\beta-n}{w},\frac{\beta}{w})
-norms.
Indeed, from Hölder inequality and Corollary
4.11
, we infer that,
for any
Q\in\mathscr{D}
and
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\leq\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\lesssim\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e}).

Hence,
\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})\approx\rho_{\textit{\L}^{w}(Q,V)}(\mathfrak{e})
.
Together with Lemmas
2.3
and
6.7
,
this implies the desired claim.

Theorem 15.3
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
Suppose that
\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
is a family
of
(u,a,b,c)
-norms for some
u\in(0,1]
and

a,b,c\in[0,\infty)
. Then

(i)

\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\eta)

is independent of the choice of the Littlewood–Paley function
\eta
.

(ii)

S_{\varphi}:\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\tilde{\varphi})\to\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)
is bounded,
where
\tilde{\varphi}(\cdot):=\varphi(-\cdot)
denotes the reflection of
\varphi
.

(iii)

T_{\psi}:\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)\to\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\varphi)
is bounded.

(iv)

If, in addition,
\varphi
and
\psi
satisfy

(15.3)

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{\varphi}(-2^{j}\xi)\hat{\psi}(2^{j}\xi)\equiv 1,\quad\forall\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\},

then
T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}
acts as the identity on
\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\varphi)
.

The assumptions, and hence the conclusions, hold in particular if

\rho_{Q}=\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q;V)}
, where

V
is an
(r,\beta)
-doubling weight.

Note that the last claim of Theorem
15.3
is immediate from Remark
15.2
.
Before going to the proof of Theorem
15.3
,
we now apply this theorem and the relationships between
average and pointwise-weighted spaces studied in Section
14

to obtain the
\varphi
-transform characterization of
pointwise-weighted spaces.

Theorem 15.4
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
V\in\bigcup_{r\geq p}\mathscr{A}_{r}
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

(i)

If
q\in(0,\infty]
, then

\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\eta)

is independent of the choice of the Littlewood–Paley function
\eta
and the operators

S_{\varphi}:\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\tilde{\varphi})\to\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(V)\quad\text{and}\quad T_{\psi}:\dot{b}^{s}_{p,q}(V)\to\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)

are bounded. Furthermore, if
\varphi
and
\psi
satisfy (
15.3
),
then
T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}
acts as the identity on
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)
.

(ii)

If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{r}
with some
r\in[p,\infty)

has optimal reverse Hölder lifting index
\varepsilon_{V}
and if
q\in(0,r+\varepsilon_{V})
,
then all the conclusions of (
i
) hold with
B
and
b

replaced by
F
and
f
.

Proof.

Assume that
V\in A_{r}
with
r\geq p
and the doubling dimension
\beta\in[n,\infty)
.
From Remark
15.2
(
ii
),
we infer that
\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

is a family of
(\min\{1,r\},\frac{n}{r},\frac{\beta-n}{r},\frac{\beta}{r})
-norms.
Hence, all the conclusions of Theorem
15.3
hold
for
\rho=\{\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(Q,V)}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
.
On the other hand, under the assumptions of the present theorem, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\cdot\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(V,\varphi)}&\approx\|\cdot\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)},\varphi)}\quad\text{by Theorems \ref {thm:equiB} and \ref {thm:equiF}},\\
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(V)}&\approx\|\cdot\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho_{\textit{\L}^{r}(\mathscr{D},V)})}\quad\text{by Corollary \ref {cor:equib} and Theorem \ref {thm:equiF}}.\end{split}

This proves the present theorem.
∎

Now, we provide the necessary ingredients for the proof of Theorem
15.3
.
We first record an easy but useful estimate:

Lemma 15.5
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. If
\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

is a family
of quasi-norms, then for any
R\in\mathscr{D}
and any sequence
t=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}

of vectors in
\mathfrak{X}
,

\rho_{R}(t_{R})\leq\ell(R)^{s}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)}.

Proof.

By dropping all but one summand in the definition, we find that

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)}\geq\Big\|\ell(R)^{-s}\rho_{R}(t_{R})\frac{\mathbf{1}_{R}}{|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}}=\ell(R)^{-s}|R|^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{R}(t_{R}),

which completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next, we need another quasi-norm of average-weighted spaces.
For any
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
, define

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\sup,\varphi)}

\displaystyle:=\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(\mathscr{D}_{j})}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}}

and

\displaystyle\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\sup,\varphi):=\Big\{f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X}):\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\sup,\varphi)}<\infty\Big\}.

Proposition 15.6
.

For any family of quasi-norms
\rho

and any
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathfrak{X})
, we have

\|S_{\varphi}f\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)}\leq\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\sup,\tilde{\varphi})}.

Note that the bound here is just “
\leq
” instead of the typical “
\lesssim
”, reflecting the relatively elementary nature of this estimate.

Proof of Proposition
15.6
.

\langle f,\varphi_{Q}\rangle=\int f(y)|Q|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi\Big(\frac{y-x_{Q}}{\ell(Q)}\Big)\,dy=|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}f*\tilde{\varphi}_{j}(x_{Q}),

where
\tilde{\varphi}(z):=\varphi(-z)
is the reflection. Thus

\begin{split}\frac{\rho_{Q}(\langle f,\varphi_{Q}\rangle)}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\rho_{Q}(f*\tilde{\varphi}_{j}(x_{Q}))\leq\sup_{y\in Q}\rho_{Q}(f*\tilde{\varphi}_{j}(y))=\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(f*\tilde{\varphi}_{j})\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(\mathscr{D}_{j})}(x)\end{split}

for all
x\in Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}
. It follows that

\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\rho_{Q}(t_{Q})\frac{\mathbf{1}_{Q}}{|Q|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(f*\tilde{\varphi}_{j})\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(\mathscr{D}_{j})}

pointwise, and hence

\|S_{\varphi}f\|_{\dot{a}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho)}\leq\Big\|\Big\{2^{js}\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(f*\tilde{\varphi}_{j})\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(\mathscr{D}_{j})}\Big\}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{A}}=\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\sup,\tilde{\varphi})}.

This finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 15.7
.

Let
\rho=\{\rho_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}}
be a family of

u
-norms with some fixed
u\in(0,1]
independent of
Q
.
Suppose that
\rho
satisfies
(
14.3
) for some
N\geq 0
, all
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathfrak{X}\setminus\{0\}
,
and all cubes
Q,R\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
of equal size
\ell(Q)=\ell(R)
.
Then

(15.4)

\|\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(\mathscr{D}_{j})}\lesssim\big(\phi_{j}*\rho_{\mathscr{D}_{j}}(\varphi_{j}*f)^{u}\big)^{\frac{1}{u}},

where
\phi(z)=(1+|z|)^{-M}
,
M>0
can be taken as large as we like,
and the implicit positive constant is independent of
f
. Moreover,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\sup,\varphi)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s}_{p,q}(\rho,\varphi)},
